# Progress Tracker

## AI Location Director — agreed upcoming approach (2026-10-31)

**Documentation agreement complete. Feature implementation: Planned / Not Started.** Reviewed the product/context rules, current V3 architecture/runtime notes, historical Location workflow and current Location prompt/config/worker boundaries. Current V3 has direct reference-conditioned Location image editing and technical validation; a structured Blueprint, physical-fit gate, mandatory four-view Location grid, semantic cross-view check and targeted correction are not implemented.

Agreed sequence: approved generated Character/optional Product sheets + latest saved creative direction/Visual Style/content Type → existing configured director selects a story/product/action-appropriate location → structured Location Blueprint (environment/rationale, layout, lighting, required space and camera positions) → feasibility validation before image generation → existing configured image model produces one empty-location 2×2 sheet (top-left aerial/top, top-right horizontal eye-level, bottom-left elevated wide-angle, bottom-right POV) → consistency check/bounded targeted correction → approved Blueprint/sheet pair saved for Storyboard/package/video reuse. All views share one physical environment and exclude Character, featured Product and unrelated people. Selection is never random aesthetics; approved Character/Product work remains unchanged.

Reuse existing `OPENAI_V3_DIRECTOR_MODEL` and `OPENAI_V3_IMAGE_MODEL` with no new model/configuration. [Canonical Location contract](location-reference.md#ai-location-director--agreed-upcoming-v3-approach-2026-10-31), [V3 architecture](generation-architecture-v3.md#ai-location-director--planned-2026-10-31), architecture context and AI workflow rules are synchronized. Historical M7 model alternatives are historical, not V3 selections. Only these five documentation files change in this unit; existing application work remains intact. No code, migration/configuration, live data, provider, worker, media generation or deployment is changed/run. Passed all six new section links/anchors, five explicit planned-status checks and whitespace checks; before/after file hashes confirm only the five intended documentation files changed. Implementation, schema design, partial-edit capability, tests and provider/visual acceptance remain pending under a later request.

## Studio file-picker cancel and V3 route config regression fixes (2026-10-30)

The native file input's bubbling `cancel` event reached the Sources dialog's unconditional `onCancel`, closing the shared Sources tree even when it was the permanent desktop grid rail. The dialog now handles only events targeted at itself. Both Character and Product/Prop use the same existing file-input component; an empty `change` now returns before `onAdd`. Picker cancellation never invokes Sources close/scroll restoration, changes references/project state or needs resize recovery. Actual dialog cancellation retains its existing close/focus/scroll cleanup. No additional body locks, viewport calculations, resize handlers or upload state were introduced.

Removed only `export const runtime = "nodejs"` from `app/api/ugc/v3/route.ts`, following installed Next.js 16 Cache Components documentation. GET/POST are its only remaining exports; private/no-store headers, origin/auth guards and default server Node.js execution remain. No V3 architecture, paid logic, providers, database/schema/migrations, historical V1/V2 data or environment values changed in this fix.

Focused checks: ESLint passed for the three code files; isolated checks of the actual JSX handlers reproduce the old descendant-cancel closure and verify the target guard, genuine dialog cancellation, empty-selection no-op and real-file callback. The existing local dev server compiled POST `/api/ugc/v3` and returned the expected origin-only 403 with `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and `CDN-Cache-Control: no-store`, before any auth/database/generation action. Both local/example Higgsfield flags remain explicitly `0`. A second dev launch detected the already-running server; it was left running and used for the check. No actual upload, browser suite, provider call, live database operation, worker, migration or deployment ran. Manual native-picker/layout and real-upload retests remain pending.

**Documentation unit complete.** The new normal AI providers are OpenAI (request understanding, Character/reference generation/regeneration, professional editable director Storyboard and provider-neutral creative instructions) and Higgsfield (native audiovisual generation, initially preferred Seedance 2.5 where supported). FFmpeg stays local. Compatible videos ≤30s normally target one continuous generation with all timed scenes/shots; longer examples target 45s→30+15, 60s→30+30, 90s→30+30+30. These are intended capability-dependent plans, not verified Higgsfield API limits or expanded current product options.

One generated clip goes directly to final review. When multiple clips are needed, users review/approve each segment before FFmpeg joins them in Storyboard order, followed by final review/approval → Complete. Native supported dialogue/audio/lip-sync/ambience/SFX replaces normal separate ElevenLabs/Kling/master-audio stages. FFmpeg handles joining only multiple clips plus optional technical metadata/duration/format/compatibility work, not creative voice/lip-sync assembly. Video costs/retries are per Higgsfield Generation Segment; preserve finite authorization, immutable intents/fingerprints, successful outputs and reconcile unknown paid outcomes before another request. Joining failure retries only local composition.

**Planned development/testing default: 480p.** Future testing/free→480p, standard→720p, premium→1080p are illustrative quality ideas only; no plan pricing, tariff, entitlement or runtime configuration changes. Exact Higgsfield model/endpoint, durations, native audiovisual/reference/output limits and pricing remain Needs confirmation; earlier fal.ai API/rate evidence does not verify Higgsfield.

**Next testing cycle:** brand-new Sceenyk project, different user-provided image, fresh Character/Product/Location/Storyboard/package/segment data, clean generation state and fresh authorization/accounting. No migration, reuse, resumption or dependency on the previous project/execution/assets/voice/master audio/paid authority. Historical projects/assets remain untouched. The earlier V2 next phase to build an ElevenLabs master-audio timeline is superseded; next eligible separately authorized work is V3-specific contract/capability verification and source planning for this fresh-project flow.

## M10 composition-owned transition correction (2026-10-28)

GET-only inspection found one current approved package (revision 1 / command version 6), total 10000ms with two contiguous 5000ms scenes. Scene 1's exact string is **`Dissolve into the next shot.`**; Scene 2 is **`Fade out cleanly.`**. Both match its frozen source and live approved Storyboard revision 5/version 8. Reported these values before changing source. The rejection came from `planning.ts:compositionIssues`, a global cut/none-only composition policy, not Seedance or lip-sync.

Added provider-neutral `CompositionCapabilities`/`CompositionRequirements`/`CompositionPlan` and signed `composition-transitions-v2` profile; preflight separately reports video, voice, lip-sync, composition and funding issues with precise scene/requested transition/supported types/layer. Known cut/fade/crossfade/dissolve names normalize; non-cut durations must be explicit and 40ms-frame aligned. Crossfade overlap must already be approved in source intervals; durations minus overlap must equal approved final duration. No guessed duration, padding, frozen frames, stretching or sound omission. Current M9's contiguous input schema cannot represent positive crossfade overlap and is unchanged.

FFmpeg renders explicit fades/xfade dissolves and mixed cut chains with matching frame rate/time base, while dedicated speech stays at absolute approved offsets. Worker verifies needed filters/encoders/FFprobe before paid intents; final source counts/timings/streams/duration are revalidated. Normalized plans persist in final-operation JSON, bound by existing package/configuration/ordered-scene hashes. Only canonical composition transition metadata is removed from the fal adapter's translated prompt; approved package/Storyboard/prompt/hash and all visual/action/camera instructions stay intact. Adapter/profile versions change configuration hashes: restart app/worker and refresh consent; existing executions are never silently upgraded. No migration/private environment change.

Current package **cannot be reused as-is**: both transition durations are absent; Scene 1 has no approved dissolve overlap; six required music/effects/ambience bindings are absent; both existing free-form voice deliveries fail the unchanged v2 mapping; and estimate **13310000 micro-USD ($13.31)** exceeds **7000000 ($7)** authorized, with **8 requests / 8 allowed**. Video/lip-sync pass. No requirement/cap was reduced or budget raised. Owner-approved edits require Save/reapproval and a newly prepared/approved package. A fixed-10s compatible option is Cut between the 5s clips and an explicitly timed final fade; retaining a dissolve needs an approved overlap/handle-capable input contract. Required sounds need an owned-audio integration or an explicit owner decision to change those requirements.

Passed **80 pure transition/profile/preflight checks**, **29 native FFmpeg transition checks** (measured fades/dissolves, exact 10s duration, absolute audio placement, mixed cuts, invalid-plan rejection), existing silent/spoken native media fixtures, **87 funding checks**, TypeScript and focused ESLint. The canonical result/hash matches a correctly named authorization exactly; no authorization value or private environment file is rewritten. Customer-credit billing remains disabled; request/estimate bounds, stage fingerprints/attempts and SQL spending gates are unchanged. Startup and runtime logs now expose only constant funding reasons and allowlisted invalid field/rule names; the final-only CLI reports `final_video_configuration` rather than a false database phase.

Read-only live Supabase OpenAPI inspection confirms all five 069 tables/columns and four expected exposed RPC signatures, including checkpoint's optional payload. Migration 069 source explicitly requires `funding=operator_funded` and accesses `ceilings[kind]`, where the operation kind is `lip_sync`; no customer-credit funding function is required. REST metadata does not expose function bodies, trigger bodies, RLS policies or establish full runtime acceptance. No corrective migration is needed for this local configuration failure; 069 remains unchanged and was not reapplied.

Added `scripts/check-final-video-config.ts` as a **network-disabled, no-runner, no-RPC** check for local DB settings, full operator authorization and private video/voice credential presence. The actual unchanged `.env.local` now returns `UGC_FINAL_VIDEO_CONFIGURATION_READY`, with funding/limits/canonical ceilings/credential configuration validated and provider/account access explicitly untested. `scripts/check-final-video-funding.ts` covers alias/hash equivalence, invalid/ambiguous limits, safe diagnostics and preserved full-package cost/request admission. Only focused configuration/static checks and GET-only database metadata inspection are authorized in this fix; no worker, provider/API generation, media processing, project/approved-asset/package mutation, remote SQL or deployment.

Validation passed: **87 offline configuration/preflight checks**, the actual environment's network-disabled configuration check, TypeScript, focused ESLint and whitespace review. No broader SQL/media/provider/build/browser suite ran for this correction.

Before restarting: optionally use canonical `lip_sync` in private JSON (never both spellings; preserve all amounts), run `node --env-file=.env.local --conditions=react-server --import=tsx scripts/check-final-video-config.ts`, restart the local app so it uses the same source/configuration, then manually restart the final-only worker when ready for its separately authorized execution. ## Storyboard speech planning before dialogue (2026-10-27)

**Complete in source; focused verification passed, manual provider/runtime acceptance pending.** Added pure `speech-budget.ts`, `prompt.ts` and `correction.ts`, integrated only into new-request provider context and the existing bounded worker correction. Default 1/2/5 scene beats cover exactly 5/10/30s; every default beat reserves 24% action/camera, 12% reaction and 4% transition, leaving <=60% speech windows with another 200ms/utterance pause allowance. Short corrected scenes reserve at least 700ms for action (capped to scene length); no available speaking time means omit speech, never create a zero-length utterance. Natural-pace planning ceilings are 6/12/35 words for the default layouts, computed from actual intervals. Other paces and corrected layouts derive their own limits. Silence may use unused speech windows for a longer realistic demonstration; dialogue cannot consume reserved reaction/transition time. The single existing correction gets exact failed scene/line interval, duration, maximum/current words/characters and recomputed scene plan; only speech-budget failures preserve valid scene structure, mixed failures use full contract correction. Existing server/SQL validators, schema, two-request budget, local-only recovery, approved references, review/edit/delete, accounting and hosted state remain unchanged. New tables enable RLS/no browser policies; service role has SELECT and the explicit RPC allowlist, no direct row writes. SECURITY DEFINER RPCs use empty search paths. Added `lib/ugc-jobs/contract.ts`, `worker-service.ts`, `server.ts`, `runner.ts`, `local-runner.ts`, and `scripts/ugc-worker.ts`, typed existing database contracts and `npm run worker:ugc` (no dependencies added). Shared constants/graph control status/stage/messages; SQL validates adjacent worker progression and state pairs. Generic advance cannot admit confirmed video or mark output complete: those future gates require their accounting/output milestones. The portable `UgcJobRunner` has no default stage handlers, so a manually enqueued/claimed job stops as `STAGE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` at `validate_inputs`, preserving that resume boundary without fake validation/AI output or automatic retry. Complete readiness belongs to Milestone 3.

Atomic SKIP LOCKED claims issue fresh tokens and 90-second leases with 20-second heartbeats; every worker update checks token/expected stage/live lease under lock. Serialized heartbeat/state RPCs avoid stale local stage overwrites. `LocalJobRunner` polls every 5 seconds, one job at a time, and waits 15 seconds after database failures. Each poll recovers at most 25 expired claims/25 unavailable queued Projects. Safe pre-provider validation alone requeues after 5 seconds within the same three-attempt budget; exhausted work fails safely. Later uncertain stages require reconciliation, not blind paid replay. Graceful shutdown releases safe work; old claim holders are fenced out. These concurrency/recovery mechanisms are authored, not runtime-tested in this task.

Server-only `enqueueOwnedUgcJob` authenticates independently, locks/verifies the owned draft UGC Project and creates/reuses the same owner/key job/snapshot; cross-Project key reuse conflicts. Snapshot is explicitly `not_validated`, includes only current brief/settings/prompt narration and retained owned reference IDs, and is immutable despite later draft edits. `getOwnedUgcJobProgress` returns only safe owned status/stage/messages/timestamps, excluding provider/storage/snapshot/claim internals. `requestOwnedUgcJobCancellation` immediately cancels queued/awaiting work, or persists a running cancellation request for a cooperative safe boundary/recovery; completed/failed jobs cannot cancel. `retryOwnedUgcJob` permits only explicit known-safe eligible failures without resetting attempts. No public action/HTTP route or Studio wiring added; these are infrastructure services for later use. No provider requests/assets/charges are implemented, and their later deduplication must build on this identity with per-stage provider/output/accounting gates.

**Render / Inngest remain disabled for current development:** confirmed source guards plus `SCEENYK_LOCAL_JOBS_ONLY=1` in local/example configuration. The local worker imports neither execution stack and bypasses signed legacy media endpoints, AI, storage writers and financial services; its entry refuses hosted opt-out and recognized Render/Vercel environments. Existing keys/URLs, Blueprint/Dockerfiles, Inngest definitions/transport/ingress guards and history are preserved. No hosted account setting, deployment, sync, job or call was changed/run/audited. The worker was not started and no test jobs were created.

## Remote loading audit (2026-10-21)

Source audit covers every App Router page/layout/loading entry, major async component consumers, and all `connection()` calls. Existing request-bound services remain unchanged. This map records source behavior, not hosted/browser acceptance.

| Surface | Remote dependency and loading behavior |
| --- | --- |
| `/projects` | Local `OwnedProjects` Suspense contains query params, Clerk and owned project/status/media/Template reads. Reuses the reference compact preview/title/date/action skeleton and real heading. |
| `/templates` | Existing async Templates child and identity fence now use compact saved-Template skeletons with the actual gallery grid. Heading/New Template stay visible. |
| `/templates/[listingId]` | Existing owned-data boundary and client identity fence use square preview/two-column detail skeleton. |
| `/settings/templates` | Existing Listings boundary and identity fence use management-card skeletons: preview, title/description, counters, actions. |
| `/settings/templates/new` | Existing NewListing boundary and client fence use the real two-column Project-picker shape. No GET/prefetch creates a draft. |
| `/settings/templates/[listingId]` | Existing OwnedListing and ListingEditor identity boundaries use form/configuration-preview skeletons. |
| `/marketplace` | URL/filter handling is separate from nested `DiscoveryResults`, which owns the actual database call below Suspense. Initial filters/gallery and subsequent gallery-only fallbacks match the real UI. |
| `/marketplace/[listingId]` | Existing Detail boundary uses square preview/detail composition. Related templates have their own four-card fallback. Existing unavailable/error handling stays intact. |
| `/marketplace/creators/[creatorSlug]` | Existing Storefront boundary uses avatar/profile/statistics and the actual published-card grid. |
| Public/managed infinite galleries | First-load card compositions match their real grids. Pending next batches append three cards inside the same grid; existing items remain visible. Existing controllers, next-page refs, response validation, identity checks, deduplication, observer root and error retry remain. |
| Settings Account / Privacy | Existing server account reads and client Clerk initialization use account/security-card skeletons. Shared static section wrappers retain actual privacy cards/copy outside the pending content. |
| Settings Creator Profile | Existing owned-profile read and client fence use name/bio/slug fields plus the avatar/upload section. Reuses its real static section header/links. |
| Settings Creation Preferences | Existing server read and client fence use the two-field form skeleton; the static captions/narration information remains real content. |
| `/credits` | Existing CreditAccount boundary uses balance/packs/purchase/activity composition. Client wallet loading replaces only pending balance/history values, preserving real pack choices/copy and existing loaded refresh content. Errors remain errors. |
| Credits/top-up dialog | CheckoutOptions shows matching financial content only while its actual remote read is pending. Existing error/check controls and checkout/reconciliation behavior remain. |
| `/credits/earnings` | Existing earnings/withdrawals boundary and client fence use summary, destination/amount form, withdrawal history, earnings totals and history skeletons. Existing refreshes retain loaded figures. |
| `/marketplace/purchases` | Existing purchases boundary and client fence use purchase-history cards. Paid adoption's initial summary request has a compact dialog skeleton; money actions retain existing progress UI. |
| `/studio` | Guest/local values remain local; actual Clerk initialization and signed-in creation-default reads use the real StudioLayout with Sources/Stage/Properties skeleton content. |
| `/projects/[id]` | Existing OwnedWorkspace boundary and client identity initialization use the same StudioLayout. Server ownership and existing failure/unavailable views remain. |
| Project/Template previews | Signed-source requests show bounded preview skeletons until settled; project/Template images/videos also cover their actual media load. Failed/absent media returns the existing abstract artwork. No private URL/cache policy change. |
| Studio media / final video / captions / scenes | Actual source/API reads use existing bounded preview/control slots; errors remove placeholders and preserve existing refresh/playback/caption controls. Generation progress is not a fetch skeleton. |
| Free allowance / paid balance | Initial remote values use inline muted placeholders; real results/errors and existing refresh mechanisms remain. |
| `/admin/payouts`, `/admin/payouts/[id]` | Existing queue/detail data boundaries use filter/row or provider/detail/history compositions. Shared chrome is outside the unchanged support authorization guard; no private children render before authorization. |
| Account/internal layouts | Shared WorkspaceShell/PageFrame stay mounted outside the server guard; route-aware presentation fallbacks reserve the selected gallery or Settings/Credits center. Guard calls, redirects, synchronization failures and privileged authorization remain. |
| Root/public/workspace layouts and account controls | Audited. Existing small Clerk account/action placeholders remain; the sidebar account placeholder now matches avatar/name rows. Root AuthProvider and static shell composition have no new remote data read. |
| Landing / Documentation / local Settings | No new skeletons: landing and Documentation are local content; topic params/metadata resolve local definitions. Appearance is local theme state, Notifications/Data are static explanations. No placeholder is added around immediately available content. |
| Compatibility redirects / API handlers / Server Actions | Audited as entry points/consumers. Redirects and data-only HTTP handlers need no visual skeleton. Their UI consumers own pending presentation. No handler, schema, service contract or action behavior changes. |

`connection()` remains in `ensureAppUser`, Marketplace public/owned request helpers, creator request helpers and the private/public preview handler. Their page callers are behind local boundaries; the preview HTTP handler is represented by media-loading UI. No `use cache`, cache flag/headers change, blocking-navigation opt-out, provider execution or hosted Inngest work is introduced.

Suspense cannot observe asynchronous work begun in client effects/events. Those reads deliberately use their existing pending/settled guards; uploads, generation processing and save/payment/withdrawal actions retain meaningful progress rather than replacing the whole view. Static content, redirects and API-only responses do not need Suspense skeletons. Shared primitives/compositions do not fetch data or introduce timers.

Validation: focused TypeScript/lint and targeted React server-render/streaming checks only. Browser dimensions, perceived motion, themes, mobile overlays and scroll appearance remain for manual review. No full build, broad regressions, browser/screenshot suites, deployment acceptance or hosted workflows.

**Complete within local production/browser/visual acceptance. Deployment: Not performed. Phase 2: Not Started.** Dashboard-first Home is removed from the main navigation. ### Architecture and migration

Mobile uses 16px blur and a 28px fade. Light/dark resolve from the existing background token; no glass-card outline, radius or new palette is introduced.

## Landing-page visual refinement and motion (2026-10-17)

**Complete within local production-build, browser and visual acceptance. Deployment: Not performed; deployed acceptance remains pending.** This closes only the user's landing-page motion/responsiveness/refinement unit. No Studio, Marketplace, Settings, private-project or broader workspace redesign is started. Inngest Cloud remains paused; no hosted workflow, generation, purchase, withdrawal or payout is triggered.

The checkout started with the earlier split hero/feature-list landing and ten supplied, untracked PNGs in public/images. Home now uses those original filenames for a layered image collage, the requested two-line headline, an explicitly illustrative Studio showcase, four visual Marketplace inspiration cards, three free/pay-as-you-go/creator presentation panels, a discovery invitation, final CTA and clean footer. The supplied light/dark landing PNGs were visually compared with actual browser captures. Existing official logos, Inter typography, semantic colors/light/dark and accessible primitives are preserved. Reference-only creator identities/listings, a $10/100-credit production offer and newsletter subscriptions are not fabricated; working discovery/account destinations replace unsupported actions. The illustrated editor/player is labeled a concept, not a real output or redesigned Studio.

Implementation is isolated in components/landing and the public home composition. PublicNavbar/PublicFooter choose landing chrome only at /; /create and /marketplace retain their original shared Navbar/Footer, verified in the actual browser. All other application pages, lib/domain services, actions/APIs, migrations, workers, auth/theme configuration, global tokens, package versions and private cache rules remain unchanged. New explicitly namespaced landing.css uses a small typed class map because the existing CSS loader emits global CSS; no shared compiler configuration is changed. Public artwork path segments are percent-encoded so the supplied punctuation/spacing works with production image optimization, without renaming/recreating assets.

Hero motion uses independent 0.35–1 depth multipliers with at most 13px horizontal/9px vertical transforms, frame-rate-independent easing and smooth return to exact resting geometry. Its frame loop stops when settled, offscreen or hidden. Fine-pointer desktop only; touch/coarse pointers and reduced motion remain static. Original resting size, position, rotation and overlap are retained during movement. The lead artwork is eager/high priority; other hero images and noncritical media use native lazy loading, avoiding hidden mobile-image requests. Responsive Next Image sizes and fixed geometry prevent image-induced layout changes. Major sections use one IntersectionObserver/Web Animations entrance with no scroll listener/perpetual loop; Pricing/Footer stay simple. Mobile has a compact navbar, real Clerk sign-in outside the navigation drawer's focus trap, hamburger navigation, adapted static collage, preview-led showcase, visual grid and stacked pricing.

## Settings implementation — Complete (2026-10-16)

The current Settings unit implements the shared authenticated dashboard shell at /dashboard/settings with account, creator-profile, credits, earnings, creation, notifications, appearance, privacy and data sections. /settings and /dashboard/settings open Account. Old profile/credits/earnings routes redirect while retaining all query parameters. Existing creator, credit purchase and financial components/services remain the source of truth; top-up returns, action invalidations and pagination target Settings. General header/summary AI balances and navbar/dashboard/admin theme toggles are removed. Contextual generation balances/cost review/top-ups remain.

Creation defaults persist only aspect ratio and category through new migration028 (user-applied; real save/reload and two-account isolation verified in Production), service-only owner-scoped RPCs, strict validation and verified Clerk identity. New signed-in drafts load defaults before the editor mounts; saved/adopted projects retain their own values. Missing persistence reports standard-default fallback honestly. Captions/styles are per-generation and voice remains centrally configured; account defaults for those require separate compatible persistence/rendering scope. No configurable notification delivery exists; Notifications has truthful information without switches. Export/deletion are unavailable. Account and Privacy delegate to Clerk; before opening account controls the server disables the authenticated user's Clerk deleteSelfEnabled flag, enforced by Clerk rather than CSS, so no incomplete deletion shortcut is offered. No user is deleted.

Theme selection reuses sceenyk-theme, root initialization, favicon and the existing root controller, adding Light/Dark/System selection plus same-tab events and in-memory behavior when storage is blocked or unwritable. Appearance radios remain disabled in the streamed server markup until hydration is ready, preventing an early selection from appearing applied without persistence. Settings HTML/RSC/prefetch/segment responses use private/no-store while public caching stays unchanged. No hosted Inngest workflows are triggered. Local, built-app and scoped Production acceptance pass; the deployed result above is current. Existing approved-contract statements below are approval history, superseded for the implemented scope by this entry.

Public account controls already have that gate. No suppression, provider/dynamic configuration, loader/action/ownership, financial/generation or cache-policy change is introduced. **Targeted cache investigation/correction (2026-10-16; local and deployed verified):** the complete profile dependency trace finds no `use cache`, `unstable_cache`, `force-cache` or positive `revalidate` on the owned read path. `ensureAppUser()` calls `connection()` before Clerk authentication/profile lookup; the Supabase adapter explicitly uses `cache: "no-store"`. The runtime Proxy predicate also missed those sibling paths. The correction extends only the private profile's header rules and runtime predicate in `next.config.ts` and `proxy.ts` to generated HTML/RSC/prefetch/segment representations. ## Historical unit — approved Marketplace earnings and withdrawal policy (2026-10-14)

**Complete — documentation/product contract only.** The approved production rules are recorded in the canonical [accounting policy](accounting.md#approved-production-marketplace-earnings-and-withdrawal-policy-2026-10-14) and synchronized across overview, project overview, architecture, payments, pricing and UI context. The workflow's current-authorization note is corrected because it previously called the now-approved fee/holding rules unapproved. AGENTS.md and code standards are unchanged. Existing acceptance scripts and pre-task documentation changes are preserved; no application, database, provider, environment or runtime change is made.

| Approved production rule | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| Marketplace platform fee / creator share | **10% / 90%**, calculated server-side from authoritative USD cents and frozen per sale. |
| Sale availability | Valid verified creator net enters Available Earnings unless blocked by an existing refund/reversal. No automatic seven-day sale hold. |
| Minimum withdrawal | **$5 USD**; server validates sufficient unreserved available balance and $5 ≤ requested amount ≤ that balance. |
| Reservation | Accepting a request immediately and atomically removes its amount from Available and records it once in Pending Payout. |
| Holding period | **Seven days from the withdrawal-request timestamp**, never the sale timestamp. No PayPal send during the hold. |
| After the hold | Revalidate refund/dispute/reversal blockers before eligibility; trusted future PayPal processing and verification precede Paid. |
| Refund/reversal interaction | Preserve cumulative proportional creator/fee adjustments, exact original-cent full reversal, no duplicate effect and immutable/auditable history, including affected reserved payout money. |
| Balance protection | Reserved money cannot fund another request; retries, concurrency and refresh/reopen retain one reservation. Pending Payout is never displayed as available. |
| AI-credit boundary | Earnings/withdrawals are USD money; AI credits remain separate, nonwithdrawable generation units. |

The documented future payout lifecycle is `requested` → `pending` → `eligible` → `processing` → `paid`, with `failed` for verified failure; `cancelled`/`reversed` are optional if later needed. Browser status claims cannot mark payouts paid. Detailed reservation allocation/reduction/cancellation, failure release, recipient/charge handling, dispute blockers and paid-out liability remain explicit questions for implementation, without reopening the approved business values.

**Validation:**12 added relative documentation links/anchors resolve, all16 canonical policy terms are present, contradictory fee/sale-hold placeholders were reviewed, and git diff --check passes. Hash checks confirm AGENTS.md, code standards and both pre-existing acceptance/test scripts are unchanged. No application test/build or provider call is needed for this documentation-only update.

**Implementation status:** existing Sandbox default 0% fee, frozen default 300-second test sale hold, immutable financial history and completed settlement/reversal acceptance below remain unchanged. The new production fee/available-on-sale policy, withdrawal tables/reservations/API/UI/scheduling, payout accounts and PayPal Payouts are **unimplemented**. Policy approval is not production rollout or payout acceptance. Earlier Marketplace unit descriptions retain historical evidence; this contract supersedes their unresolved fee/share/minimum/hold placeholders.

**Exact next recommended Marketplace unit:** creator withdrawal reservations, seven-day eligibility and trusted server-side **PayPal Sandbox payouts under the approved policy**, including versioned production-rule application that preserves historical fee/eligibility snapshots, refund interactions, ownership, idempotency, recovery and real acceptance. Do not start it automatically. Live changes, multi-currency payouts and final-renderer work are excluded. **Final rendering remains In Progress/deferred separately for the existing Render Free RAM limit.**

## Approved Marketplace product / architecture decision (2026-10-13)

**Documentation contract complete; implementation status is recorded in the current unit above.** Creators explicitly publish a reusable, creator-owned listing derived from an owned successful project/result. Discovery and free/template adoption create a separate adopter-owned project containing only safe reusable configuration. The creator keeps the original project and listing; no private media, R2 assets, signed URLs, secrets, private metadata or unlicensed content transfers. Publishing requires rights confirmation; server/database access must enforce project/listing ownership and listing access. The simple planned lifecycle is draft, published, unpublished, archived. Moderation, reports, takedowns and IP complaints need a later contract.

Creator adoption price and estimated Sceenyk AI generation credits must be displayed separately. Free adoption does not waive normal generation eligibility/cost. Paid adoption later requires verified purchase and durable entitlement before creating the adopter-owned project. Creator earnings are monetary and separate from nonwithdrawable AI credits. Future sale accounting needs pending/available/lifetime earnings, platform fee, settlement/holding, refund/chargeback/dispute handling and eventual payout history. **Creator payouts and PayPal Payouts are deferred.** No automatic per-sale transfer, Marketplace capture, settlement automation or moderation is authorized here.

**Updated production approvals:**10% platform fee /90% creator share, USD earnings/withdrawals, minimum withdrawal $5, immediate reservation and seven-day hold from the withdrawal request are approved by the 2026-10-14 contract above. Production listing-price bounds, recipient/charge handling, detailed refund/dispute allocation/recovery and reusable private-asset rights remain future questions. Enforce the approved share server-side in the future production rollout; existing snapshots remain unchanged. Existing pay-as-you-go two lifetime free generations, one-time credit packs and server-quoted credit accounting remain unchanged. Final rendering remains **In Progress/deferred** because Render Free RAM is insufficient; this decision does not claim a publishable rendered result exists.

**Original approved implementation scope (now delivered above):** Marketplace UI + explicit publishing/listing persistence + browse/search/detail + free/template adoption that creates an adopter-owned project, with server-enforced ownership/privacy and rights confirmation. Specify acceptance around eligible completed results, safe snapshot/preview, listing lifecycle, duplicate adoption, two-account isolation and ordinary generation quote handoff. Paid adoption, earnings settlement, creator payouts, moderation, ratings/reviews, subscriptions, auto-recharge and final-render changes are later units. Do not start implementation automatically.

**Documentation verification:** eight context documents changed, including the canonical overview and its project-overview pointer. The diff contains only documentation; `git diff --check` and relative Markdown target checks pass. No application runtime test, migration, payment call, render or deployment was performed for this documentation-only decision.

## Approved product / architecture decision — pay-as-you-go credits (2026-10-13)

**Documentation/product-contract update: Complete.** The primary business model is **"Buy credits. Create what you need. Top up when you need more."** Users retain two lifetime free generations under the existing duration/entitlement rules, then buy credit packs, consume credits through the quoted generation lifecycle and manually top up when their balance is low or zero. Longer/paid requests still follow the existing server entitlement rules.

Credits are usage units, not monthly allowances. A pack does not promise a month of access or a recurring plan. **Purchased credits do not expire unless a future explicit product decision changes this.** Generation cost remains visible before explicit confirmation; pricing, eligibility, balances and reservation amounts remain server-authoritative. Existing reserve/consume/restore behavior is unchanged. Five-minute quote expiry is not purchased-credit expiry. Larger packs may eventually offer better value per credit; no production rates or discount schedule are approved by this decision.

**Monthly subscriptions, monthly credit allocations and recurring plans are not current scope or the next required commercial unit.** Possible future membership benefits—discounted credits, priority generation, premium models, larger uploads and higher-quality exports—are examples only, not approved scope or promised entitlements. **Auto-recharge is a separate optional future feature:** explicitly authorized payment method + user-selected threshold/predefined pack → automatic pack purchase below that balance threshold. Consent/authorization, charge limits, failure recovery and implementation need a future specification; no auto-recharge is implemented here.

**Existing implementation/status preserved:** PayPal Sandbox one-time purchases are **Complete within Merchant Sandbox acceptance**, including the real USD 1.00 → 100-credit exactly-once result below. Authenticated purchases, immutable purchase ledger, owned balance reads, authoritative quotes, free allowance and reservation/settlement remain implemented. Production pack prices, generation tariffs, provider-to-credit economics, markup and any larger-pack value schedule remain unresolved. Historical subscription/monthly-plan assumptions below are retained for provenance and **superseded** by this approved contract.

**Final rendering remains In Progress / deferred:** Render Free 512 MB RAM is insufficient for hosted Remotion/FFmpeg final rendering; hosted acceptance waits for a separately authorized plan upgrade. No rendering attempt, application feature, code/schema/environment/deployment change or payment was performed in this documentation task. Transitions support none/cut at 0ms, with missing values defaulting to cut; other instructions persist unsupported-transition conflicts. No looping/freezing/stretching/truncating/speech regeneration/automatic extension occurs. Ready plans/checks and conflicting plans are immutable; duplicate claims/completions reuse the same row and cached checks. Existing holds remain reserved for both ready and conflict outcomes. ### Owned source preparation and timing normalization — scoped real acceptance complete (2026-10-12)

Historical acceptance below used the former bearer-token transport; current HMAC/Render setup and its verification limits are recorded above.

- **Boundary/timing:** `lib/media-preparation/` and the standalone authenticated `scripts/media-preparation-worker.ts` run before new video transform/reuse. Inngest durably sends only owned scene/job/run IDs over bounded HTTP; FFmpeg is never imported into Next handlers or browsers. Immutable v1 seconds convert to canonical integer milliseconds on new scene creation. Strict timing and actual FFprobe duration checks prevent invalid/reversed/inconsistent/overrun segments from reaching paid submission. Existing text/image execution and historical completed/requested scenes are retained.
- **Schema/lifecycle:** user applied `20261012001200_prepared_source_segments.sql`; its hosted table/columns and claim/complete/fail RPCs are exercised live. All twelve migrations apply in isolated PostgreSQL. The narrow derivative cache links existing owned source/job/scene identities and records selected/original timing, original/prepared bytes/geometry, normalization/version, private key/MIME/ETag and timestamps. Composite ownership FKs, browser-denied RLS/grants, immutable completed cache and a DB paid-slot gate are tested. Cache identity includes source ID/ETag, range and normalization hash. Five-minute leases serialize preparation, three attempts bound transient retries, and existing conditional R2 outputs recover interrupted completion. No duplicate upload/accounting system is added; applied migrations 001–011 remain unchanged.
- **Normalization/overlays:** central v1 settings produce fast-start H.264/yuv420p MP4, 24fps, square pixels, upright geometry and no audio. Same framing is retained/downscaled without enlargement; another plan aspect fits/pads without cropping. Suitable full-range video is remuxed, including dropping audio without recompressing video; subsets transcode accurately at CRF18. Actual rotated/portrait/padded fixtures pass. ### Earlier Gemini implementation — Complete within development/hosted-service acceptance

### Commercial cost contract — Complete within scoped development/test and deployed core acceptance

- **Pricing boundary:** `lib/pricing/` owns validated server-only retail rules, workload resolution and the authenticated quote service; `app/actions/pricing.ts` is the thin request boundary. Credits are internal usage units with no assumed dollar, PayPal or provider conversion. Existing creation schemas/choices are reused. Exact workload rules support duration, category, transformation/new generation, trusted model tier, quality and expensive features; current model/quality routing is explicitly unassigned/workspace-default. No provider cost or secret is exposed.
- **Quote model/UI:** immutable owner/project/request/input-bound quotes contain exact free/credit decision, required credits, balance/allowance snapshot, mode/version and five-minute expiry. Review does not reserve. Explicit confirmation accepts only quote ID. Edits require another review; safe stale/unavailable/insufficient states and required/available credit copy are real. Buy credits stays disabled/coming soon. Test pricing is visibly labeled; light/dark tokens/layouts are unchanged. Cached-route activation clears cancelled quote busy state and recovers uncertain confirmations.

**Durable dispatch evidence and boundaries (2026-10-10):**

- Technology: Inngest SDK 4.23.0, official test engine 1.0.0 and real Dev Server CLI 1.46.0 with persistent SQLite. One coordinator; `/api/inngest` uses official signature verification in cloud mode and bypasses interactive Clerk only for that exact route. Production forces cloud mode, even if the local dev opt-in is accidentally deployed.
- Schema: user-confirmed application of `20261010000500_generation_dispatch.sql`; fresh hosted metadata includes all seven dispatch/claim fields. Existing migrations 001–004 and auth/project/media schemas are preserved. Service-only RPCs reserve/acknowledge attempts, load/validate/claim jobs, fail owned claims and reconcile. Browser roles cannot invoke them; direct service table writes cannot supply claim/dispatch metadata. Full hosted SQL catalog/grant audit remains separate; authored grants/constraints pass isolated PostgreSQL.
- Delivery: job commits queued/pending before send. Event `sceenyk/generation.requested` contains only `generationJobId`, with stable job/attempt event ID. A failed/uncertain send preserves the original row. Two-minute DB reservation cooldown and conditional acknowledgement cover concurrent sends, process exit and ack/claim races; worker receipt is proven separately.
- Worker: atomic authoritative job/project/application-user/assets verification and claim using the Inngest run ID. Another run exits; the same run resumes after an uncertain DB response. Four supported retries handle temporary failures; malformed/missing/integrity failures are non-retryable. No client status mutation or arbitrary dispatch action exists.
- Recovery: minute cron considers at most 25 oldest queued jobs, including pending/failed and accepted-but-unclaimed attempts. Separate durable per-row steps prevent one failed send from blocking the batch. A fifteen-minute safety timeout fails stale preparing claims without transferring ownership/replaying expensive work. Service downtime preserves outbox rows; active cron registration and operational monitoring are required when it returns.
- Honest boundary: actual worker sets processing/preparing, sleeps durably one minute, then records safe failure because no production pipeline is connected. It never marks completed or invents output. Workspace copy explains this; current UI uses real persisted stage/status without percentages. Future provider work must replace this handoff and revisit timeouts/provider idempotency.

Stale expiry uses time-aged isolated fixtures; no fifteen-minute hosted crash soak was performed.

### Historical media goal

Implement only authorized upload → R2 storage → Supabase metadata → owned project association → private retrieval. Required variables: `R2_ACCOUNT_ID`, `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `R2_BUCKET_NAME`; optional `MEDIA_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`. No public credentials. Temporary single-PUT safeguard defaults to 100 MiB, centrally configurable up to R2's 5 GiB single-PUT ceiling; this is not a final commercial limit. See [media-storage.md](media-storage.md).

### Media unit status and intentional limitations

- Storage: configured real `sceenyk` R2 bucket; browser OPTIONS/PUT/private GET, object HEAD/list, signed constraints, expiry and replay refusal verified. Public access disabled per user confirmation; exact local CORS origin verified.
- Database: `supabase/migrations/20261009000300_project_assets.sql` is applied by the user; hosted metadata writes/reads/finalization/retry exercised. Composite owner/project FK, request/key uniqueness, RLS/browser denial and restricted grants tested against the actual migrations in isolated PostgreSQL. Full hosted catalog/grant audit remains unperformed without SQL/public-role access.
- Upload: server resolves Clerk → application user → owned project before authorizing five-minute direct PUT. Declared MIME/category/size validated; finalization checks storage size, Content-Type and supported file signatures before uploaded status. Conditional writes prevent overwrite using a replayed upload URL.
- Retrieval: fifteen-minute owned read URLs for verified assets; project reopen restores metadata, active source requests fresh access. No permanent blob URL identity. Pending uploads can be checked after refresh.
- Retry: same local upload request reuses one metadata row; concurrent/duplicate finalization is safe. Transient upload failures leave pending metadata; invalid stored content becomes rejected.
- Deletion/limits: local selections can be removed. Permanent deletion, abandoned pending/rejected-object cleanup, resumable multipart uploads, final product limits, deeper decoding/moderation and production abuse quotas remain later decisions/work.
- Verification: lint, standalone TypeScript, production build and privileged-secret client scan pass. **42 real-browser/hosted-Supabase/R2 checks plus 20 live signing/storage security checks pass**, alongside 80 isolated SQL/source/SDK checks. Earlier 35 development failure checks and 24 simulated checks remain historical evidence, not substitutes for these live results.
- Next small unit: finish the separate broader owned-project acceptance; a first generation-job contract may be scoped afterward with new authorization. No generation/job implementation begins here.

## Verification

### Live media acceptance (2026-10-10) — Complete in development

- Traced real selected File → owned project save/authorization → pending row → returned signed URL/headers → XHR `send(File)` → R2 → HEAD/ranged content verification → uploaded row → local list/private preview → refresh/reopen. Initial R2 list was empty. Chrome DevTools protocol recorded OPTIONS 403 and `PreflightMissingAllowOriginHeader`; a header matrix proved `Content-Type` alone passed and adding `If-None-Match` failed. After the user's CORS fix, actual OPTIONS 204, PUT 200 and private GET 200/206 pass. No transport proxy, simulated R2, fake asset REST or disabled CORS is used in this run.
- **42 live browser checks:** PNG/WebM/WAV selection and real direct transfers; unsaved project-first persistence; explicitly injected transport interruption leaves pending metadata/Files; retry uses the same row; deliberately lost finalization request leaves actual R2 bytes pending; subsequent retry verifies without another PUT. Three assets match R2 HEAD size/type/ETag, private image/video/audio previews decode, repeat finalization is idempotent, refresh and leave/reopen restore assets. Desktop/mobile 1440/390/320px fit both themes; screenshots inspected. Invalid MIME/size and supplied owner/key are rejected server-side. B cannot authorize/list/finalize/read/open A's media/project; B's own real upload/object/refresh pass. Signed-out actions deny all four media operations. Zero uncaught browser exceptions.
- **20 live storage/security checks:** actual action response binds content-length/type/host/if-none-match and five-minute expiry; retry reuses one hosted asset; missing object cannot finalize; Check upload sends no PUT and reports absent storage. R2 returns 403 `SignatureDoesNotMatch` for changed type, byte count, key or removed signed condition; untampered browser File PUT/finalization succeeds. Replay returns 412 `PreconditionFailed`. Actual read payload expires after fifteen minutes; unsigned S3 GET returns 400 `InvalidArgument` with no media. Authentic SDK PUT/GET signatures dated beyond their 300/900-second lifetimes return 403 `ExpiredRequest` (no system-clock change or production expiry override). Correctly sized falsely declared PNG bytes upload to R2 but finalization rejects their unsupported signature and denies application preview. Verified bytes remain unchanged; the real bucket independently lists five objects under the final A test project (three media, one security PNG and one intentionally rejected test object).
- Bucket r2.dev/custom-domain public access is disabled per the user's settings confirmation. Object credentials cannot administer/read bucket CORS; the successful exact-origin preflight and denied other-origin probes establish observed CORS behavior. This is not an independent Cloudflare account-settings audit.
- Lint, standalone TypeScript and production build pass; 80 isolated checks pass again. Twenty production client JS files contain no configured Clerk/Supabase/R2 privileged secrets or server database markers. Temporary development diagnostics contained stage/status only and were removed after verification. Existing migrations, ignored environment credentials and framework configuration remain unchanged; `next dev` restored the enabled generated feedback instructions in AGENTS.md.
- Final refresh after diagnostic removal restores four uploaded assets and the intentionally rejected object; the private image decodes when brought into view, with zero uncaught exceptions. The final viewing check accounts for the native image's lazy loading. The isolated browser and verification server are stopped.
- Temporary evidence: `C:\Users\USER\AppData\Local\Temp\sceenyk-r2-reproduction.json`, `sceenyk-r2-{browser,cors,acceptance,security}.cjs`, and `C:\Users\USER\AppData\Local\Temp\sceenyk-clerk-live-x3oA5C\{real-r2-acceptance.json,real-r2-security.json,real-r2-*.png}`. Harness corrections addressed a preview status counted as an extra asset and a transient null document during reload and case-sensitive matching of CSS-capitalized validation text; these were test-harness issues. Reserved development fixtures and deliberately pending/rejected rows/objects are retained; no user data was deleted.

## Architecture Decisions

- **Approved 2026-10-17:** creative-studio-first UI/UX direction; public/workspace environments; Studio naming and guest draft/auth handoff; four-area desktop Stage-first layout; progressive tablet collapse; mobile Stage + Properties with separate full-screen Sources and hamburger navigation; visual-first discovery; unchanged branding/tokens/Settings/server/financial contracts. Redesign implementation Not Started; first unit is Phase 1 — shared public/workspace shells and navigation architecture. Cloud remains paused.

- **Complete within deployed two-user acceptance,2026-10-16 (`c2987f1`):** public creator storefront, owner-only profile management, stable unique slugs, safe avatars, published-only projections and correct links. Both blockers are resolved:14 profile loads,24 cache/isolation probes and71 full checks pass. Public/client scans are clean, protected state is unchanged and no other feature starts.

- **Approved 2026-10-14:** Marketplace platform fee10%, creator share90%; verified valid creator earnings become available unless blocked by existing refund/reversal. USD minimum withdrawal $5, immediate idempotent reservation into Pending Payout and a seven-day hold from the withdrawal request. No payout during the hold; after eligibility checks, only the future trusted PayPal payout flow can verify Paid. Preserve cumulative proportional reversals and immutable history. This is documentation approval, with withdrawal/payout implementation still unstarted.

- **Approved 2026-10-13:** explicit creator-owned Marketplace listings are reusable templates separate from original projects. Free adoption creates a new adopter-owned project from safe configuration, with ordinary generation credits separate. Free/paid Sandbox adoption and settlement/reversal now have separate completed acceptance. Production fee/share 10%/90%, USD minimum $5 and seven-day withdrawal-request hold are approved by the 2026-10-14 contract; withdrawals and payouts remain future implementation. Server ownership/privacy and creator rights confirmation are mandatory.
- **Approved 2026-10-13:** primary monetization is pay-as-you-go credit packs and manual top-ups, with two separate lifetime free generations, non-expiring purchased credits unless explicitly changed later, and visible server-authoritative cost before confirmation. No month of access, monthly allocation, subscription prerequisite or recurring plan. Membership and auto-recharge are separate unapproved future options; no implementation is authorized by this decision.

- Keep one root-level Next.js/Tailwind/shadcn/Base UI design system. Preserve server composition where practical; approved landing pointer/scroll and Studio interactions stay in scoped client boundaries. Composition changes do not replace identity/tokens or services.
- Use the existing token palette in both themes. Default to system preference with a light CSS fallback; explicit choice persists under `sceenyk-theme`, is applied before paint, and synchronizes across tabs.
- Application identity uses one server-only Supabase SDK layer and a unique Clerk ID upsert. Supabase Auth and browser database access are unused; future private resources require independent verified identity and ownership checks.
- Clerk is the only identity source. Use official centered modals/UserButton, development keys only, neutral loading, and server guard checks at proxy/layout/page boundaries. Missing configuration denies dashboard access. No browser-supplied identity/return URL, shared auth cache, or custom auth system.
- Public pricing and media concepts are explicit UI previews; they do not establish billing configuration, generated content, or backend capacity.
- Workspace local Files/options stay in component state; active local previews own/revoke temporary object URLs. Explicit R2 uploads require an owned persisted project and server storage verification; restored private assets use fresh read URLs. Browser preview checks remain advisory, separate from server upload validation.
- All payment development/testing uses **PayPal Sandbox only**. No Live credentials, Live checkout, or real-money transactions. Eventual production environment selection belongs behind the payment service and is not implemented here.
- Preserve planned service boundaries: Supabase for structured data, object storage for media, background execution for long AI/rendering work, and server-authoritative ownership/credits with ledger-backed idempotency.

## Update Rules

After each meaningful implementation unit: record actual behavior, changed files, validation evidence and limitations, incomplete work, migrations/configuration needs, resolved decisions, and the next bounded unit. Move work to Complete only after its scoped acceptance checks pass. UI-only work cannot complete a feature requiring backend enforcement. Keep context and source synchronized and distinguish preferred/installed/configured/verified integrations.
